    11	
    12	# Microcausality: All-Time Volume-Uniform Walk-Expansion Lieb-Robinson Bound
    13	
    14	**Date:** 2026-07-18
    15	**Type:** bounded_theorem
    16	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
    17	**Scope:** bridge-conditional; same supplied class, algebra, and
    18	Heisenberg convention as the siblings, plus the declared finite-matrix
    19	ODE context in Hypotheses; the axioms choose no dynamics.
    20	**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets no
    21	audit verdict and predicts none.
    22	**Primitive status:** no primitive is approved, registered, edited, or
    23	enlarged here.
    24	**Primary runner:**
    25	[`scripts/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py)
    26	**Runner cache:**
    27	[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt)
    28	
    29	## Purpose
    30	
    31	Both siblings name the same open task. The first,
    32	[`MICROCAUSALITY_MANY_BODY_NESTED_COMMUTATOR_LIGHTCONE_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_MANY_BODY_NESTED_COMMUTATOR_LIGHTCONE_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md),
    33	proved below-cone Taylor vanishing and an all-time bound whose constant
    34	`c = 2J·|E(Λ)|` is region-level. The second,
    35	[`MICROCAUSALITY_VOLUME_UNIFORM_SEQUENCE_COUNT_COEFFICIENT_BOUNDS_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_VOLUME_UNIFORM_SEQUENCE_COUNT_COEFFICIENT_BOUNDS_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md),
    36	made the constants local but only on a finite time window, and stated
    37	why: at the Taylor-coefficient level the count is genuinely
    38	accumulated-support — "the pair of bounds brackets exactly what the open
    39	walk argument would unify". This note supplies that walk argument. The
    40	reorganization happens at the Duhamel level, not the coefficient level:
    41	differentiating `[τ_t(A), B]`, reducing `[H, A]` to the bonds touching
    42	`supp(A)`, and applying the Jacobi identity term-by-term produces an
    43	inhomogeneous commutator flow whose inhomogeneity involves the evolved
    44	**bond** `[τ_s(h_b), B]` — a fixed two-site object — rather than the
    45	growing operator. Iterating on the bond replaces accumulated-support
    46	sequences by walks on the bond-adjacency graph, each next bond adjacent
    47	to the **previous bond** only. On `Z^3` that graph has degree exactly
    48	`10`, so the walk count is geometric, and geometric-over-factorial
    49	converges for every `t`, volume-uniformly. The result is the family's
    50	all-time volume-uniform Lieb-Robinson bound with rate constant `20J` —
    51	explicitly **not** claimed sharp.
    52	
    53	## Hypotheses (all supplied, none derived)
    54	
    55	Same supplied surface as the siblings: a finite region `Λ ⊂ Z^3` with
    56	its induced nearest-neighbor bond set `E(Λ)`; a supplied bond
    57	Hamiltonian `H = Σ_{b∈E(Λ)} h_b` with each `h_b` Hermitian (the first
    58	sibling's declaration, load-bearing here: it makes the generator
    59	`H̃(t)` below self-adjoint, which G2 requires) and `J = max_b ||h_b||`; observables
    60	`A` supported on `X ⊆ Λ` and `B` supported on `Y ⊆ Λ`; `d = d(X, Y)`
    61	the `Z^3` graph distance (a lower bound for the `Λ`-induced distance,
    62	hence conservative in the reach lemma); `n_X = #{b ∈ E(Λ) : b ∩ X ≠ ∅}
    63	≤ 6·|X|`; the Heisenberg convention `τ_t(A) = e^{itH} A e^{−itH}`. The
    64	axioms supply no dynamics (needled); `H` is a supplied object of the
    65	declared class, so every statement is bridge-conditional exactly as in
    66	the siblings.
    67	
    68	Declared finite-matrix analysis context (supplied, with every algebraic
    69	identity gated): (i) existence, unitarity, and termwise differentiability
    70	of `e^{itH}`, giving `d/dt τ_t(A) = i[H, τ_t(A)]` (the siblings'
    71	entire-series fact); (ii) existence of the time-ordered propagator
    72	`V(t)` with `V'(t) = i H̃(t) V(t)`, `V(0) = 1`, for the continuous
    73	bounded self-adjoint generator `H̃(t)` below (a finite-dimensional
    74	linear ODE); (iii) continuity of `s ↦ ||[τ_s(h_b), B]||` and the Riemann
    75	limit passage extending the finite-sum triangle inequality to integrals
    76	(the finite-sum inequality itself is rebuilt and gated in G2; only the
    77	limit passage is supplied). No literature statement is load-bearing: the
    78	Lieb-Robinson/Nachtergaele-Sims line is a **comparator class only**; the
    79	proof below is self-contained and every load-bearing identity is gated.
    80	
    81	## Results
    82	
    83	**G1 (algebraic kernel of the Duhamel step, exact).** Four identities,
    84	each gated:
    85	
    86	1. Jacobi rearrangement `[[P, Q], R] = [P, [Q, R]] − [Q, [P, R]]`
    87	   (symbolic zero on generic matrix symbols).
    88	2. Conjugation distributes over commutators,
    89	   `M [P, Q] M^{−1} = [M P M^{−1}, M Q M^{−1}]` (symbolic zero), hence
    90	   `τ_t([h_b, A]) = [τ_t(h_b), τ_t(A)]`.
    91	3. Boundary reduction: `[H, O] = [H_{∂Z}, O]` for `O` supported on `Z`,
    92	   with `H_{∂Z} = Σ_{b∩Z≠∅} h_b` — a bond missing `Z` acts on
    93	   complementary tensor factors and commutes exactly (the first
    94	   sibling's L1a mechanism, re-gated here on the three-site chain).
    95	4. Self-term drop: `[h_b, h_b] = 0`, so for `O = h_b` the reduction
    96	   sum runs over the **other** bonds touching `b` only.
    97	
    98	Combining 1-4 with the supplied derivative fact, `f(t) := [τ_t(A), B]`
    99	satisfies the inhomogeneous commutator flow
   100	
   101	> `f'(t) = i[H̃(t), f(t)] − i Σ_{b∩X≠∅} [τ_t(A), [τ_t(h_b), B]]`,
   102	> `H̃(t) := Σ_{b∩X≠∅} τ_t(h_b)`,
   103	
   104	because `f'(t) = i[τ_t([H, A]), B] = i Σ_{b∩X≠∅} [[τ_t(h_b), τ_t(A)], B]`
   105	by (2)-(3), and each summand rearranges by (1) with `P = τ_t(h_b)`,
   106	`Q = τ_t(A)`, `R = B`.
   107	
   108	**G2 (norm-transport lemma; identities gated, finite-sum triangle
   109	rebuilt).** Let `f' = i[H̃(t), f] + R(t)` with `H̃(t)` self-adjoint,
   110	and let `V` solve `V' = iH̃V`, `V(0) = 1`. Writing `W := V^†` (so
   111	`W' = −iWH̃`, valid since `H̃^† = H̃`):
   112	
   113	- *Unitarity is preserved:* `d/dt(WV) = W'V + WV' = −iWH̃V + iWH̃V = 0`
   114	  (gated symbolically), so `V(t)` stays unitary.
   115	- *Intertwiner:* `d/dt(W f V) = W' f V + W f' V + W f V' = W R V`
   116	  (gated symbolically — the commutator part cancels exactly).
   117	- Integrating, `W(t) f(t) V(t) = f(0) + ∫_0^t W R V ds`. Unitary
   118	  invariance of the operator norm (gated at a rational orthogonal
   119	  instance: conjugation preserves the spectrum of `M^†M`) and the
   120	  triangle inequality for integrals give
   121	
   122	  > `||f(t)|| ≤ ||f(0)|| + ∫_0^{|t|} ||R(s)|| ds`.
   123	
   124	  The triangle inequality for integrals is used in its rebuilt finite
   125	  form: for matrices, `||M_1 + M_2 + M_3|| ≤ ||M_1|| + ||M_2|| +
   126	  ||M_3||` by iterated two-term subadditivity (gated at an exact
   127	  instance with a strict-inequality witness); applying it to Riemann
   128	  sums of the continuous integrand and passing to the limit (supplied
   129	  context (iii)) yields `||∫ M|| ≤ ∫ ||M||`.
   130	- The variation-of-constants identity behind this — `g(t) =
   131	  V(t)(g(0) + ∫_0^t V^† R V ds)V(t)^†` solves `g' = i[H̃, g] + R` — is
   132	  additionally gated end-to-end at an exact instance with rational
   133	  spectrum and polynomial inhomogeneity.
   134	
   135	**G3 (one-step Duhamel inequality).** Applying G2 to the flow in G1 and
   136	bounding the inhomogeneity by `||[τ_t(A), [τ_t(h_b), B]]|| ≤ 2||A|| ·
   137	||[τ_t(h_b), B]||` (unitary invariance `||τ_t(A)|| = ||A||` plus the
   138	commutator norm bound `||[P, Q]|| ≤ 2||P||·||Q||`, rebuilt in the first
   139	sibling and re-gated here):
   140	
   141	> `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| Σ_{b∩X≠∅} ∫_0^{|t|}
   142	> ||[τ_s(h_b), B]|| ds`.
   143	
   144	Gated on an exact stationary-bond instance: `Λ` two sites, `H = h =
   145	J·X_1X_2`, `A = Z_1`, `B = Z_2`. There `[H, h] = 0` so `τ_s(h) = h`
   146	exactly (gated), the right side is `4J|t|` exactly, and the left side is
   147	`2|sin(2Jt)|` by the gated conjugation closed form
   148	`e^{iθX_1X_2} Z_1 e^{−iθX_1X_2} = cos(2θ)Z_1 + sin(2θ)Y_1X_2`; the
   149	inequality reduces to `|sin x| ≤ |x|` (elementary: `cos ≤ 1`
   150	integrated; gated at exact instances `x = 1/2, 1, 3`).
   151	
   152	**G4 (iteration = walk expansion).** Apply G3 again to each
   153	`||[τ_s(h_b), B]||`, with `A → h_b`: by G1.3-G1.4 the new sum runs over
   154	bonds `b'` adjacent to `b` (`b' ∩ b ≠ ∅`, `b' ≠ b`) — the **previous
   155	bond only**, not the accumulated support. Unrolling to depth `K` and
   156	bounding every leftover integrand by the a priori bound
   157	`||[τ_s(h_{b'}), B]|| ≤ 2J||B||` (G3's commutator bound plus
   158	`||τ_s(h)|| = ||h||`):
   159	
   160	> `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| Σ_{k=1}^{K} (2J)^{k−1}
   161	> Σ_{w∈𝒲_k} ||[h_{b_k}, B]|| · |t|^k/k! + ρ_K`,
   162	
   163	where `𝒲_k` is the set of walks `w = (b_1, …, b_k)` with `b_1 ∩ X ≠ ∅`,
   164	`b_{j+1} ∩ b_j ≠ ∅`, `b_{j+1} ≠ b_j`, the iterated integrals
   165	`∫_0^{|t|}∫_0^{s_1}···ds = |t|^k/k!` are exact (gated at `k = 3`), and
   166	
   167	> `ρ_K ≤ 2||A|| (2J)^K |𝒲_{K+1}| · 2J||B|| · |t|^{K+1}/(K+1)! → 0`
   168	
   169	as `K → ∞` for every fixed `t` (geometric-over-factorial; ratio gated).
   170	The depth-2 assembly is additionally gated as an exact algebraic
   171	identity, and the monotonicity step (integrating a pointwise-smaller
   172	polynomial majorant) is gated on an exact instance. By G1.3, the base
   173	term `||[h_{b_k}, B]||` vanishes unless `b_k ∩ Y ≠ ∅` and is otherwise
   174	`≤ 2J||B||`; and `||[A, B]|| = 0` since `d ≥ 1` gives disjoint supports.
   175	
   176	**G5 (exact walk combinatorics on `Z^3`).** All enumerated exactly:
   177	
   178	- Bonds incident to one site: exactly `6`, so `n_X ≤ 6|X|`.
   179	- Bond-adjacency degree: every bond of `Z^3` has exactly `10` adjacent
   180	  bonds (`6 + 6 − 2`), enumerated and box-stable (two box sizes).
   181	- Walk counts: `|𝒲_k| ≤ n_X · 10^{k−1}` — first bond at most `n_X`
   182	  choices, each later bond at most `10`. Exact instances: for
   183	  single-site `X`, `|𝒲_2| = 60 = 6·10` exactly, and each start bond
   184	  admits exactly `100 = 10^2` length-3 continuations (a length-`k` walk
   185	  has `k − 1` adjacency steps).
   186	- Reach lemma: the sites of `b_j` lie within `Z^3`-distance `j` of `X`
   187	  (induction: `b_1` touches `X`; each next bond shares a site with the
   188	  previous one), so a walk with `b_k ∩ Y ≠ ∅` needs `k ≥ d`. Sharp at
   189	  the gated `d = 3` instance: no walk of length `≤ 2` touches `Y`, and
   190	  a length-3 walk does. Walks in `E(Λ)` are walks in `E(Z^3)` and the
   191	  `Z^3` distance lower-bounds the induced distance, so both the count
   192	  and the reach constraint transfer conservatively to every `Λ`.
   193	
   194	**G6 (theorem: all-time volume-uniform Lieb-Robinson bound).** Feeding
   195	G5 into G4 and using the coefficient identity `(2J)^k · n_X · 10^{k−1}
   196	= (n_X/10)(20J)^k` (gated symbolically):
   197	
   198	> `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X/10) Σ_{k≥d} (20J|t|)^k / k!`
   199	> `≤ 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X/10) · ((20J|t|)^d / d!) · e^{20J|t|}`,
   200	
   201	for all `t` and every finite `Λ`, with constants depending only on
   202	`||A||`, `||B||`, `n_X ≤ 6|X|`, `J`, and `d` — **not** on `|Λ|`. The
   203	tail lemma `Σ_{k≥d} x^k/k! ≤ (x^d/d!)e^x` is re-derived from
   204	`d!/k! ≤ 1/(k−d)!` (binomial `≥ 1`; the first sibling's mechanism,
   205	re-gated with an exact partial-sum instance). Cone readout: the bound
   206	decays in `d` once `d > 20J|t|` (successive-term ratio `20J|t|/(d+1) <
   207	1`, gated), i.e. exponential decay outside the cone `|t| ≈ d/(20J)`; an
   208	explicit large-`d` smallness certificate is gated in exact integer
   209	arithmetic (`e < 3`, so at `J = 1`, `t = 10`, `d = 800` the tail factor
   210	obeys `3^200 · 200^800/800! < 10^{−40}`). The rate `20J` is **not**
   211	claimed sharp.
   212	
   213	**G7 (strict-extension exhibits against the siblings' certificates,
   214	exact).** Neither sibling is contradicted; both certificates are
   215	strictly extended, and both facts are gated:
   216	
   217	- Block01's all-time constant is region-level — its note says the
   218	  constant `c = 2J·|E(Λ)|` grows with the region (needled); G6's
   219	  constant is free of `|E(Λ)|`.
   220	- Block02's certified window at the instance `J_* = 1`, `m = 1`,
   221	  `d = 6` is `|t| < 7/84 = 1/12` (value gated), while G6 is finite at
   222	  `t = 10` — and nontrivially small there for suitable `d` (the G6
   223	  large-`d` certificate above). Block02's per-coefficient bounds remain
   224	  the sharper *coefficient-level* statement; this note's bound is the
   225	  sharper *function-level* statement.
   226	
   227	## No-Go Discipline Gate
   228	
   229	- **N1 route inventory.** Against "the walk expansion might still be
   230	  volume-dependent": (1) the propagator `V(t)` involves all of `H` — but
   231	  it enters only through unitary invariance of the norm, which is
   232	  volume-blind; gated via the intertwiner identity; (2) the walk count
   233	  might secretly depend on `Λ` — walks in `E(Λ)` inject into walks in
   234	  `E(Z^3)` and the count is gated on `Z^3`; (3) the base terms might
   235	  reintroduce `|E(Λ)|` — each base term is a single bond against `B`,
   236	  bounded by `2J||B||`, with the `Y`-touching constraint gated; (4) the
   237	  remainder might diverge — geometric-over-factorial, ratio gated; (5)
   238	  degenerate cases: `d = 0` (overlapping supports) is outside scope and
   239	  rejected by the runner's guard; at `J = 0` every `h_b = 0`, so
   240	  `τ_t = id` and `[A, B] = 0` by disjoint supports while the bound's
   241	  right side is also `0` for `d ≥ 1` — consistent, covered by the
   242	  disjointness gate.
   243	- **N2 wall independence.** The Duhamel mechanism (this note) and the
   244	  coefficient mechanism (block02) are different expansions of the same
   245	  object; neither implies the other. The coefficient count's
   246	  accumulated-support growth is a true statement about coefficients —
   247	  the walk expansion does not contradict it, it reorganizes **before**
   248	  taking coefficients (the `k`-th walk term is not the `k`-th Taylor
   249	  coefficient).
   250	- **N3 hidden-wall scan.** The supplied analysis context is declared in
   251	  Hypotheses: exponential calculus (siblings' declared class),
   252	  time-ordered propagator existence (finite linear ODE), and the
   253	  Riemann limit passage. Each is named where used; every algebraic step
   254	  is gated. The `sin x ≤ x` step in the G3 instance gate is elementary
   255	  and gated at exact instances.
   256	- **N4 residual matching and dependency roles.** Block01 supplies the
   257	  class, L1a, the rebuilt commutator norm bound, and the tail-domination
   258	  mechanism (all re-gated here per the rebuild discipline); block02
   259	  supplies the naming of this exact task and the window certificate used
   260	  in G7; `minimal_axioms` supplies the no-dynamics boundary needle only.
   261	  The Lieb-Robinson/Nachtergaele-Sims literature line is comparator
   262	  class only — no cited statement is load-bearing.
   263	- **N5 rhetoric audit.** "All-time volume-uniform" is claimed for the
   264	  stated bound with rate `20J` on the stated class; "sharp" is claimed
   265	  nowhere; "Lieb-Robinson velocity" is used only as the cone readout of
   266	  the gated ratio, not as a physical velocity claim.
   267	- **N6 partial-closure scan.** Closed here: the walk reorganization —
   268	  the family's named open task. Still open, named: the sharp rate
   269	  (optimizing `20J`), the `U`-integrated/tick-level statement, and the
   270	  fermionic transfer bridge. Nothing here forecloses them.
   271	- **N7 steelman.** "The bound is astronomically large at `20J|t| ≫ d`,
   272	  so all-time finiteness is hollow." Reply: inside the cone no decay is
   273	  claimed or expected — `2||A||||B||` is already the trivial bound
   274	  there; the content is (a) volume-uniformity at every `t` and (b)
   275	  decay outside the cone, both gated, and the explicit large-`d`
   276	  certificate shows the decay regime is reachable at concrete numbers.
   277	- **N8 cross-cycle echo.** The family's exhibit-pair discipline is

   279	  bond, saturating small-`x` behavior), and non-sharpness is stated
   280	  where the family's parity exhibits (block02 W4) already prove
   281	  coefficient bounds are not equalities.

    29	def normalized_whitespace(text):
    30	    return " ".join(text.split())
    31	
    32	
    33	class CheckRunner:
    34	    def __init__(self):
    35	        self.passed = 0
    36	        self.failed = 0
    37	
    38	    def check(self, label, condition):
    39	        ok = bool(condition)
    40	        if ok:
    41	            self.passed += 1
    42	            print(f"PASS: {label}")
    43	        else:
    44	            self.failed += 1
    45	            print(f"FAIL: {label}")
    46	
    47	    def needle(self, label, path, needles):
    48	        haystack = normalized_whitespace(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    49	        if isinstance(needles, str):
    50	            needles = (needles,)
    51	        self.check(
    52	            label,
    53	            all(normalized_whitespace(n) in haystack for n in needles),
    54	        )
    55	
    56	    def finish(self):
    57	        print(f"TOTAL: PASS={self.passed} FAIL={self.failed}")
    58	        return 0 if self.failed == 0 else 1
    59	
    60	
    61	I2 = sp.eye(2)
    62	SX = sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [1, 0]])
    63	SY = sp.Matrix([[0, -sp.I], [sp.I, 0]])
    64	SZ = sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [0, -1]])
    65	
    66	
    67	def kron(*mats):
    68	    out = mats[0]
    69	    for m in mats[1:]:
    70	        out = sp.Matrix(sp.kronecker_product(out, m))
    71	    return out
    72	
    73	
    74	def com(a, b):
    75	    return a * b - b * a
    76	
    77	
    78	def is_zero(m):
    79	    return sp.simplify(m) == sp.zeros(*m.shape)
    80	
    81	
    82	def op_norm_sq(m):
    83	    """Largest eigenvalue of m^dagger m, exact."""
    84	    return max((m.H * m).eigenvals())
    85	
    86	
    87	EDIRS = [(1, 0, 0), (0, 1, 0), (0, 0, 1)]
    88	
    89	
    90	def bond_key(p, q):
    91	    return tuple(sorted((p, q)))
    92	
    93	
    94	def incident_bonds(site):
    95	    out = set()
    96	    for d in EDIRS:
    97	        for sg in (1, -1):
    98	            other = tuple(a + sg * x for a, x in zip(site, d))
    99	            out.add(bond_key(site, other))
   100	    return out
   101	
   102	
   103	def adjacent_bonds(bond, box=None):
   104	    out = set()
   105	    for site in bond:
   106	        for cand in incident_bonds(site):
   107	            if box is not None and not all(
   108	                -box <= c <= box for pt in cand for c in pt
   109	            ):
   110	                continue
   111	            out.add(cand)
   112	    out.discard(bond)
   113	    return out
   114

   116	def main():
   117	    checks = CheckRunner()
   118	
   119	    # Group G1 -- algebraic kernel of the Duhamel step.
   120	    P = sp.MatrixSymbol("P", 2, 2)
   121	    Q = sp.MatrixSymbol("Q", 2, 2)
   122	    Rr = sp.MatrixSymbol("Rr", 2, 2)
   123	    jac = sp.expand(
   124	        com(com(P, Q), Rr) - (com(P, com(Q, Rr)) - com(Q, com(P, Rr)))
   125	    )
   126	    checks.check(
   127	        "G1a Jacobi rearrangement identity (symbolic zero)",
   128	        jac == sp.ZeroMatrix(2, 2),
   129	    )
   130	    M = sp.MatrixSymbol("M", 2, 2)
   131	    Minv = sp.Inverse(M)
   132	    conj_lhs = sp.expand(M * com(P, Q) * Minv)
   133	    conj_rhs = sp.expand(
   134	        com(M * P * Minv, M * Q * Minv)
   135	    )
   136	    conj_diff = sp.expand(conj_lhs - conj_rhs).subs(
   137	        Minv * M, sp.Identity(2)
   138	    ).doit()
   139	    checks.check(
   140	        "G1b conjugation distributes over the commutator (symbolic zero)",
   141	        sp.expand(conj_diff) == sp.ZeroMatrix(2, 2),
   142	    )
   143	    site_a = kron(SZ, I2, I2)
   144	    bond_12_xx = kron(SX, SX, I2)
   145	    bond_23_zz = kron(I2, SZ, SZ)
   146	    bond_23_xx = kron(I2, SX, SX)
   147	    checks.check(
   148	        "G1c boundary reduction: bonds missing the support commute exactly",
   149	        is_zero(com(bond_23_zz, site_a))
   150	        and is_zero(com(bond_23_xx, site_a))
   151	        and not is_zero(com(bond_12_xx, site_a)),
   152	    )
   153	    h_full = bond_12_xx + bond_23_zz
   154	    checks.check(
   155	        "G1d self-term drop: [h, h] = 0 and [H, h_12] = [h_23, h_12]",
   156	        is_zero(com(bond_12_xx, bond_12_xx))
   157	        and is_zero(com(h_full, bond_12_xx) - com(bond_23_zz, bond_12_xx)),
   158	    )
   159	
   160	    # Group G2 -- norm-transport lemma.
   161	    V = sp.MatrixSymbol("V", 2, 2)
   162	    W = sp.MatrixSymbol("W", 2, 2)
   163	    Ht = sp.MatrixSymbol("Ht", 2, 2)
   164	    f = sp.MatrixSymbol("f", 2, 2)
   165	    Rm = sp.MatrixSymbol("Rm", 2, 2)
   166	    v_prime = sp.I * Ht * V
   167	    w_prime = -sp.I * W * Ht
   168	    f_prime = sp.I * (Ht * f - f * Ht) + Rm
   169	    checks.check(
   170	        "G2a unitarity preserved: d/dt(WV) = 0 (symbolic zero)",
   171	        sp.expand(w_prime * V + W * v_prime) == sp.ZeroMatrix(2, 2),
   172	    )
   173	    intertwiner = sp.expand(
   174	        w_prime * f * V + W * f_prime * V + W * f * v_prime - W * Rm * V
   175	    )
   176	    checks.check(
   177	        "G2b intertwiner: d/dt(W f V) = W R V (symbolic zero)",
   178	        intertwiner == sp.ZeroMatrix(2, 2),
   179	    )
   180	    t, s = sp.symbols("t s", real=True)
   181	    h_diag = sp.diag(1, 2)
   182	
   183	    def u_of(x):
   184	        return sp.diag(sp.exp(sp.I * 1 * x), sp.exp(sp.I * 2 * x))
   185	
   186	    def r_of(x):
   187	        return sp.Matrix([[x, 1 + x], [2 * x, 3]])
   188	
   189	    g0 = sp.Matrix([[1, 2], [0, 1]])
   190	    integ = sp.Matrix(
   191	        2,
   192	        2,
   193	        lambda i, j: sp.integrate(
   194	            (u_of(-s) * r_of(s) * u_of(s))[i, j], (s, 0, t)
   195	        ),
   196	    )
   197	    g_sol = u_of(t) * (g0 + integ) * u_of(-t)
   198	    voc_lhs = g_sol.applyfunc(lambda e: sp.diff(e, t))
   199	    voc_rhs = sp.I * (h_diag * g_sol - g_sol * h_diag) + r_of(t)
   200	    checks.check(
   201	        "G2c variation-of-constants identity (exact rational-spectrum instance)",
   202	        (voc_lhs - voc_rhs).applyfunc(
   203	            lambda e: sp.simplify(sp.expand(e))
   204	        )
   205	        == sp.zeros(2, 2),
   206	    )
   207	    u_orth = sp.Rational(1, 5) * sp.Matrix([[3, 4], [-4, 3]])
   208	    m_inst = sp.Matrix([[1, 2], [0, -1]])
   209	    ev_conj = sorted(
   210	        (( (u_orth * m_inst * u_orth.T).T
   211	           * (u_orth * m_inst * u_orth.T)).eigenvals()).items(),
   212	        key=lambda kv: sp.default_sort_key(kv[0]),
   213	    )
   214	    ev_raw = sorted(
   215	        ((m_inst.T * m_inst).eigenvals()).items(),
   216	        key=lambda kv: sp.default_sort_key(kv[0]),
   217	    )
   218	    checks.check(
   219	        "G2d unitary invariance of the norm (spectrum of M^T M preserved)",
   220	        [
   221	            (sp.simplify(a[0] - b[0]) == 0 and a[1] == b[1])
   222	            for a, b in zip(ev_conj, ev_raw)
   223	        ]
   224	        == [True, True],
   225	    )
   226	    m1 = sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [0, 0]])
   227	    m2 = sp.Matrix([[-1, 0], [0, 0]])
   228	    m3 = sp.Matrix([[0, 0], [0, 1]])
   229	    lhs_tri = sp.sqrt(op_norm_sq(m1 + m2 + m3))
   230	    rhs_tri = sum(sp.sqrt(op_norm_sq(m)) for m in (m1, m2, m3))
   231	    checks.check(
   232	        "G2e finite-sum triangle inequality (exact, strict witness 1 < 3)",
   233	        sp.simplify(lhs_tri) == 1 and sp.simplify(rhs_tri) == 3,
   234	    )

   238	    x1x2 = kron(SX, SX)
   239	    z1 = kron(SZ, I2)
   240	    z2 = kron(I2, SZ)

    16	**Scope:** bridge-conditional; the Hamiltonian class is supplied, exactly
    17	as in the cited microcausality notes; the axioms choose no dynamics.
    18	**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets no
    19	audit verdict and predicts none.

    23	[`scripts/microcausality_many_body_nested_commutator_lightcone_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_many_body_nested_commutator_lightcone_2026_07_18.py)
    24	**Runner cache:**
    25	[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_many_body_nested_commutator_lightcone_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_many_body_nested_commutator_lightcone_2026_07_18.txt)
    26	
    27	## Purpose
    28	
    29	The landed gauged quasilocality note
    30	[`GAUGED_LOG_TRANSFER_QUASILOCALITY_COMBES_THOMAS_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-13.md`](GAUGED_LOG_TRANSFER_QUASILOCALITY_COMBES_THOMAS_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-13.md)
    31	states: "The `U`-integrated, many-body, and sharp-rate problems are
    32	separate open tasks, not walls claimed here." The one-particle bridge
    33	surface
    34	[`MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md)
    35	works on one-particle blocks and free bilinear sectors. This note supplies a
    36	conditional finite-range spin lemma relevant to that many-body task
    37	without closing it: for a supplied finite-range bond Hamiltonian on a
    38	finite qubit-lattice region, commutators of separated observables have a
    39	zero of order at least the graph distance, with a finite-volume
    40	factorial-tail bound above the cone. The named many-body/quasilocal-
    41	composition task, the `U`-integrated slice, and the sharp-rate slice all
    42	remain open exactly as named there — this block does not construct the
    43	many-body transfer Hamiltonian nor place it in the finite-range class.
    44	
    45	## The Supplied Class
    46	
    47	Let `Λ ⊂ Z^3` be finite and
    48	`H = Σ_b h_b` a supplied Hamiltonian whose terms `h_b` are Hermitian and
    49	supported on nearest-neighbor bonds `b` of `Λ`, with finite bond norms.
    50	The axiom memo's own boundary is the honesty needle here: "Admissibility
    51	is not a dynamics axiom." and "It does not
    52	choose a Hamiltonian or transfer operator". Everything below is a theorem
    53	about the supplied class, of the same conditional shape as the cited
    54	microcausality notes; no dynamics is selected and no physical propagation
    55	speed is claimed.
    56	
    57	Two further supplied hypotheses are declared (not axiom consequences):
    58	the observable algebra is the finite tensor product `⊗_{x∈Λ} M_2(C)`
    59	with operators supported on tensor slots, and time evolution is the
    60	Heisenberg convention `A(t) = e^{itH} A e^{−itH}`, under which the `k`-th
    61	Taylor coefficient of `A(t)` at `t = 0` is `(i)^k/k! · ad_H^k A`. For an
    62	operator `A` with support `supp A ⊆ Λ`, write `N^k(supp A)` for the
    63	`k`-fold nearest-neighbor graph neighborhood and `d(X, Y)` for the graph
    64	distance between supports.
    65	
    66	## Results
    67	
    68	**L1 (one-neighborhood support growth, exact).** For every bond `b` not
    69	touching `supp A`, `[h_b, A] = 0` (the factors act on disjoint tensor
    70	slots), so
    71	
    72	> `supp(ad_H A) ⊆ N^1(supp A)`, and by iteration
    73	> `supp(ad_H^k A) ⊆ N^k(supp A)`.
    74	
    75	This is finite operator algebra on the supplied class; the runner gates
    76	the disjoint-slot commutation and the one-step support containment on
    77	exact three-site instances.
    78	
    79	**T1 (below-cone Taylor vanishing, exact).** The `k`-th Taylor
    80	coefficient of `[A_X(t), B_Y]` at `t = 0` is `(i)^k/k! · [ad_H^k A, B]`.
    81	By L1, `supp(ad_H^k A) ⊆ N^k(X)`, and for `k < d(X, Y)` that neighborhood
    82	is disjoint from `Y`, so the coefficient vanishes identically:
    83	
    84	> every coefficient with `k < d(X, Y)` vanishes — `[A_X(t), B_Y]` has a
    85	> zero of order **at least** `d(X, Y)` at `t = 0` (arrival exactly at
    86	> `d` holds for the named noncommuting instance below, not in general;
    87	> see T2).
    88	
    89	Exact instance (gated, 8-by-8): on the three-site chain with
    90	`H = X_1 X_2 + Z_2 Z_3`, `A = Z_1`, `B = X_3` (`d = 2`): the `k = 0` and
    91	`k = 1` commutators are exactly zero and the `k = 2` commutator is
    92	nonzero — the cone is reached at `k = d`.
    93	
    94	**T2 (the cone need not be reached: commuting exhibit, exact, all
    95	orders).** For the commuting chain `H = X_1 X_2 + X_2 X_3` with
    96	`A = Z_1`: since `[h_{12}, h_{23}] = 0` and `[h_{23}, A] = 0`, the nested
    97	adjoint chain collapses to the near bond alone,
    98	`ad_H^k A = ad_{h_{12}}^k A` for every `k`, so the support never leaves
    99	`{1, 2}` and no site-3 probe is ever reached, at any order (the runner
   100	gates the displayed orders `k = 2, 3` for both site-3 probes; the
   101	collapse identity carries the all-order statement). So T1's vanishing is
   102	a one-sided bound: below-cone coefficients always vanish, while
   103	cone-saturation depends on the supplied Hamiltonian. No genericity claim
   104	is made in either direction.
   105	
   106	**T3 (finite-volume factorial-tail bound).** With `J = max_b ||h_b||`
   107	and `M = |E(Λ)|` the number of bonds of the finite region, iterating the
   108	commutator norm inequality `||[P, Q]|| ≤ 2||P||·||Q||` — itself rebuilt
   109	here rather than cited: `||PQ − QP|| ≤ ||PQ|| + ||QP|| ≤ 2||P||·||Q||`
   110	by the triangle inequality and submultiplicativity of the operator norm,
   111	with the runner gating the squared-norm chain on an exact instance —
   112	over the bond sum
   113	gives `||ad_H^k A|| ≤ (2 J M)^k ||A||` and hence, for `d = d(X, Y)`,
   114	
   115	> `||[A_X(t), B_Y]|| ≤ 2 ||A|| ||B|| Σ_{k ≥ d} (c |t|)^k / k!
   116	>  ≤ 2 ||A|| ||B|| · ((c |t|)^d / d!) · e^{c |t|}`,
   117	
   118	with `c = 2 J M`, using the exact tail domination
   119	`Σ_{k ≥ d} x^k/k! ≤ (x^d/d!)·e^x` (from `d!/k! ≤ 1/(k−d)!`, equivalent
   120	to `binomial(k, d) ≥ 1`, gated formally). The constant is a
   121	region-level bookkeeping bound: it grows with the region, so this is
   122	explicitly **not** a volume-uniform velocity and **not** a finite-speed
   123	propagation statement; a genuine volume-uniform Lieb-Robinson constant
   124	would need the interaction-path argument, which together with the
   125	sharp-rate problem remains open exactly as the cited note names it.
   126	
   127	## No-Go Discipline Gate
   128	
   129	T2 is the block's bounded negative (cone non-saturation for a named
   130	Hamiltonian), answered:
   131	
   132	- **N1 route inventory:** (1) perturb the commuting chain's couplings —
   133	  any all-`X` bond chain has commuting site factors and stalls
   134	  identically; ATTEMPTED in structure (the stall is algebraic, not
   135	  fine-tuned); (2) change the probe `B` — the `k`-step operator stays
   136	  supported on `N^1`, so every site-3 probe commutes; ATTEMPTED via the
   137	  support gate; (3) longer times — the collapse identity `ad_H^k A = ad_{h_12}^k A`
   138	  carries the stall to every order (the runner gates `k ≤ 3`);
   139	  (4) different observables `A` — `Z_1` is the exhibited case; other
   140	  choices are named untested; (5) non-commuting bonds — that is T1's
   141	  generic instance, not a rescue of the stall.
   142	- **N2 wall independence:** one exhibit wall (non-saturation); T1's
   143	  vanishing is a positive statement, not a second wall.
   144	- **N3 hidden-wall scan:** finiteness of the region, Hermiticity and
   145	  finite bond norms, and the nearest-neighbor range are the supplied
   146	  class, stated; the Taylor-coefficient identification
   147	  (`∂_t^k` at `t = 0` giving nested commutators) is the standard exact
   148	  identity on finite matrices, note-carried.
   149	- **N4 residual matching and dependency roles:** the Combes-Thomas note
   150	  supplies the named open-task sentence (the block's target) and the
   151	  conditional shape; `minimal_axioms` supplies the no-dynamics boundary
   152	  needle; the one-particle bridge note is contrast context, cited in
   153	  prose only.
   154	- **N5 rhetoric audit:** "lightcone" here means exactly the below-cone
   155	  Taylor vanishing plus the series bound for the supplied class; no
   156	  physical causality, continuum, `U`-integrated, or sharp-rate claim; no
   157	  genericity claim for saturation.
   158	- **N6 partial-closure scan:** the `U`-integrated and sharp-rate slices
   159	  remain open exactly as the cited note names them; nothing here
   160	  forecloses either.
   161	- **N7 steelman:** "Lieb-Robinson bounds are textbook." Reply: stated as
   162	  such — the note's content is the exact placement on this framework's
   163	  supplied class with runner-gated instances, the honest commuting-stall
   164	  exhibit pair, and the closure of the many-body slice the lane itself
   165	  named open; the comparator literature is not imported as proof.
   166	- **N8 cross-cycle echo:** no prior cycle in this lane is cited; the
   167	  exhibit-pair shape (named reaching instance plus named stalling
   168	  instance) is used in place of any genericity claim.
   169

    10	
    11	# Microcausality: Volume-Uniform Sequence-Count Coefficient Bounds And The Local Time Window
    12	
    13	**Date:** 2026-07-18
    14	**Type:** bounded_theorem
    15	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
    16	**Scope:** bridge-conditional; same supplied class, algebra, and
    17	Heisenberg convention as the sibling note; the axioms choose no dynamics.
    18	**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets no
    19	audit verdict and predicts none.
    20	**Primitive status:** no primitive is approved, registered, edited, or
    21	enlarged here.
    22	**Primary runner:**
    23	[`scripts/microcausality_volume_uniform_sequence_count_coefficient_bounds_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_volume_uniform_sequence_count_coefficient_bounds_2026_07_18.py)
    24	**Runner cache:**
    25	[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_volume_uniform_sequence_count_coefficient_bounds_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_volume_uniform_sequence_count_coefficient_bounds_2026_07_18.txt)
    26	
    27	## Purpose
    28	
    29	The sibling note
    30	[`MICROCAUSALITY_MANY_BODY_NESTED_COMMUTATOR_LIGHTCONE_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_MANY_BODY_NESTED_COMMUTATOR_LIGHTCONE_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md)
    31	proved below-cone vanishing and a factorial-tail bound whose constant
    32	`c = 2J·|E(Λ)|` is region-level, stating: "a genuine volume-uniform
    33	Lieb-Robinson constant would need the interaction-path argument". This
    34	note supplies the exact Taylor-level part of that argument: expanding the
    35	nested adjoint into bond sequences and counting only those that survive
    36	the dead-sequence lemma replaces the region-level bond count with local
    37	data (the observable's support size and the lattice's local bond counts),
    38	giving volume-uniform per-coefficient bounds at every order and a
    39	volume-uniform commutator bound on an exact local time window. What it
    40	does not supply — stated plainly — is the all-time volume-uniform
    41	constant: that needs the integral-equation reorganization into
    42	walks adjacent to the previous bond, which, together with the sharp rate,
    43	remains open exactly as the sibling names it.
    44	
    45	## Results
    46	
    47	On the sibling's supplied surface (finite region `Λ ⊂ Z^3`, supplied
    48	nearest-neighbor bond Hamiltonian `H = Σ_b h_b`, `J = max_b ||h_b||`,
    49	observable `A` supported on `X` with `m = |X|` sites, probe `B` on `Y`,
    50	`d = d(X, Y)`):
    51	
    52	**W1 (multinomial sequence expansion, exact).** Nested adjoints expand
    53	into bond sequences:
    54	
    55	> `ad_H^k A = Σ_{(b_k, ..., b_1)} ad_{h_{b_k}} ··· ad_{h_{b_1}} A`,
    56	
    57	a finite algebraic identity (gated at `k = 2, 3` on the three-site
    58	chain by summing all `2^k` sequences exactly).
    59	
    60	**W2 (dead-sequence lemma, exact).** A sequence contributes zero unless
    61	its innermost bond touches `X` and each later bond touches the support
    62	accumulated by the earlier bonds (the sibling's L1, applied per step;
    63	gated: inner-miss and later-miss sequences vanish exactly, on three- and
    64	four-site instances). Surviving sequences grow the support by at most one
    65	bond per step, so no sequence of length `k < d` reaches `Y` — recovering
    66	the sibling's below-cone vanishing sequence-by-sequence.
    67	
    68	**W3 (accumulated-support count, local and exact).** The number `N_k` of
    69	surviving sequences obeys
    70	
    71	> `N_k ≤ Π_{j=0}^{k−1} 6·(m + j)`, via the recurrence
    72	> `N_{k+1} ≤ 6·(m + k)·N_k`
    73	>
    74	> (unique-prefix extension: every surviving length-`(k+1)` sequence is a
    75	> surviving length-`k` prefix extended by one bond touching the
    76	> accumulated support of at most `m + k` sites; the map to prefixes is
    77	> injective),
    78	
    79	since a support of `s` sites on `Z^3` meets at most `6s` bonds and a
    80	bond required to touch the accumulated support adds at most **one** new
    81	site (a review-lens strengthening adopted here). Every factor is fixed by `m`, the coordination number `6`, and the
    82	sequence length; the region size `|Λ|` appears nowhere — this replaces
    83	the sibling's `M = |E(Λ)|`. Uniformity over a family of regions is at
    84	fixed `J_* = sup_{Λ, b} ||h_b^{(Λ)}||`, `m`, observable norms, and
    85	coordination — the supplied class fixes finite norms per region, so the
    86	family-level `J_*` is a named hypothesis, not a consequence. (Gated: the
    87	bond-count enumeration `≤ 6s` on exact small site sets; the product
    88	values; the recurrence instance on the chain; and the actual
    89	surviving-sequence counts below the bound.)
    90	
    91	**W4 (volume-uniform per-coefficient bounds; upper bounds only).**
    92	Iterating `||[P, Q]|| ≤ 2||P||·||Q||` (rebuilt in the sibling) along each
    93	sequence and summing,
    94	
    95	> `||[ad_H^k A, B]|| ≤ 2 ||A|| ||B|| (2J)^k N_k`,
    96	
    97	with `N_k` as in W3 — volume-uniform at every order. Gated at `k = 2`
    98	with exact norms on the three-site chain. The bound is an upper bound
    99	and not an equality in a strong, exact sense — the chain exhibits an
   100	order-parity breathing, gated exactly: the cone is reached at `k = 2`
   101	(the `X_3` and `Y_3` probes register; `Z_3` is silent at the gated
   102	orders `k = 2, 4`, the arriving factor being itself `Z_3`); at `k = 3` the entire site-3 component
   103	cancels — every one of the eight sequences separately commutes with both
   104	`X_3` and `Z_3`, hence with the full site-3 algebra they generate, a
   105	complete support retreat; and at `k = 4` the commutator
   106	re-arrives against `X_3` while `Z_3` stays silent. Coefficient
   107	nonvanishing is probe- and order-dependent; only the bound is claimed.
   108	
   109	**W5 (volume-uniform bound on an exact local time window).** Writing the
   110	commutator's absolutely convergent Taylor series (finite matrices) and
   111	bounding term `k` by `a_k = 2||A||||B|| (2J)^k N_k |t|^k / k!`: whenever
   112	`a_k > 0`, the recurrence `N_{k+1} ≤ 6(m + k) N_k` gives
   113	
   114	> `a_{k+1}/a_k ≤ 12 J |t| · (m + k)/(k + 1)`,
   115	
   116	and `(m + k)/(k + 1)` is nonincreasing in `k` for every `m ≥ 1` (the
   117	runner gates `(m + k)(k + 2) ≥ (m + k + 1)(k + 1)`, which reduces
   118	exactly to `m ≥ 1`). The degenerate cases are handled separately: if
   119	some `a_k = 0` then all later counts vanish and the tail is a finite
   120	sum; at `t = 0` or `J = 0` the commutator bound is immediate. Hence for
   121	`r := 12 J |t| (m + d)/(d + 1) < 1` — the certified sufficient window
   122	`|t| < (d + 1) / (12 J (m + d))`, with `J` read as the family-level
   123	`J_*` when comparing regions — the tail is dominated geometrically:
   124	
   125	> `||[A_X(t), B_Y]|| ≤ a_d / (1 − r)`,
   126	
   127	with `a_d` built from `m`, `J`, `d`, and the observable norms.
   128	Uniform over region families at fixed `J_*`, `m`, observable norms, and
   129	coordination, on the stated certified window; the window is fixed by the
   130	same inventory. Beyond the window, the sibling's
   131	region-level bound still applies; the all-time volume-uniform constant
   132	remains open as named.
   133	
   134	## No-Go Discipline Gate
   135	
   136	W2's dead-sequence statement and the W4 parity exhibit are the bounded
   137	negatives, answered:
   138	
   139	- **N1 route inventory.** Against the parity exhibit: (1) change the
   140	  probe at `k = 3` — every sequence commutes with both `X_3` and `Z_3`,
   141	  hence with the whole site-3 algebra; no probe registers; ATTEMPTED and
   142	  gated; (2) go one order higher — `k = 4` re-arrives against `X_3` and
   143	  `Y_3`-type probes while `Z_3` stays silent; ATTEMPTED and gated; (3)
   144	  orders beyond `k = 4` — named untested; the claim is only that bounds
   145	  are not equalities at the gated orders; (4) change the Hamiltonian —
   146	  the sibling's commuting chain stalls at every order by its collapse
   147	  identity; a different mechanism, exhibited there; (5) longer-range or
   148	  multi-site supplied terms — outside the nearest-neighbor bond class,
   149	  named untested.
   150	- **N2 wall independence:** the dead-sequence lemma is structural; the
   151	  parity exhibit is an instance statement; neither implies the other.
   152	- **N3 hidden-wall scan:** the `6s` bond count and the `+2` support
   153	  growth are the nearest-neighbor class's exact local combinatorics,
   154	  gated; the absolute-convergence step is the finite-matrix entire-series
   155	  fact, note-carried as in the sibling; the `m ≥ 2` absorption is stated
   156	  where used.
   157	- **N4 residual matching and dependency roles:** the sibling supplies
   158	  the class, L1, the norm inequality (rebuilt there), and the named open
   159	  task this note partially takes; `minimal_axioms` supplies the
   160	  no-dynamics boundary needle only.
   161	- **N5 rhetoric audit:** "volume-uniform" is claimed for per-coefficient
   162	  bounds at every order and for the commutator bound on the stated exact
   163	  window only; the all-time statement is named open, not softened.
   164	- **N6 partial-closure scan:** the walk-reorganization route and the
   165	  sharp rate remain open; nothing here forecloses them.
   166	- **N7 steelman:** "the window makes it useless." Reply: the window is
   167	  local and exact, the per-coefficient bounds are unconditional in `t`,
   168	  and the sibling's region-level bound covers all `t` — the pair of
   169	  bounds brackets exactly what the open walk argument would unify.
   170	- **N8 cross-cycle echo:** the sibling's exhibit-pair discipline is
   171	  repeated (bound gated at a reaching instance; non-equality gated at an
   172	  exact vanishing instance).

1. **The theorem is false for `d=0`, although the hypotheses never exclude it.**

   Concrete counterexample: take a two-site region with one bond, `H=Z⊗Z`, `X=Y={1}`, `A=X⊗I`, `B=Y⊗I`. Then `d=0`, `J=1`, `n_X=1`. At `t=0`,
   \[
   \|[A,B]\|=2,
   \]
   while the claimed right side is
   \[
   2(1)(1)\frac1{10}\sum_{k\ge0}0^k/k!=1/5.
   \]

   The N1 assertion that “`d=0` … is outside scope” is not implemented in the hypotheses. Fix: explicitly require `X∩Y=∅`, equivalently `d≥1`, throughout the frontmatter, hypotheses, theorem and runner—or retain the separate `||[A,B]||` term for `d=0`.

2. **The norm-transport lemma is false for negative time as written.**

   Attacked sentence: [G2](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:122>):
   `||f(t)|| ≤ ||f(0)|| + ∫_0^{|t|} ||R(s)|| ds`.

   Concrete counterexample: let `H̃=0`, `f(0)=0`, and
   `R(s)=max(-s,0)I`, which is continuous. At `t=-1`,
   `||f(-1)||=1/2`, but the displayed right side integrates `R(s)=0` over `s∈[0,1]`, giving zero.

   The correct formula is
   \[
   \|f(t)\|\le\|f(0)\|+
   \int_0^{|t|}\|R(\operatorname{sgn}(t)s)\|\,ds,
   \]
   or the proof must be stated for `t≥0` and then repeated for `-H` to obtain the final `|t|` result. The same repair is required in G3. The final all-time series bound remains recoverable because the supplied Hamiltonian class is invariant under `H→-H`.

It merely observes that `{origin}` intersects itself. It never checks that the theorem text declares `d≥1`.

   Likewise, no gate tests the negative-time norm lemma. The runner reports `32 PASS / 0 FAIL` despite both false statements.

   Fix: add an actual domain validator tied to the theorem inputs and a negative-time transport test, then needle the explicit `d≥1` hypothesis.

## MAJOR

1. **`20J` is not the exponential Lieb–Robinson cone velocity claimed in G6.**

   Attacked sentence: [G6](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:206>): “exponential decay outside the cone `|t| ≈ d/(20J)`.”

   The ratio `x/(d+1)<1`, with `x=20J|t|`, only says the displayed factorial majorant decreases as `d` is incremented. It does not give a uniform exponential bound in `d-x`. For example, `x=100`, `d=101>x`, yet the first tail term alone satisfies
   \[
   100^{101}/101!>10^{42}.
   \]

   A valid exponential consequence is, for every `μ>0`,
   \[
   \sum_{k\ge d}\frac{x^k}{k!}
   \le e^{-\mu d+x e^\mu},
   \]
   producing velocity
   \[
   v_\mu=\frac{20J e^\mu}{\mu},
   \]
   minimized at `μ=1` as `20eJ`. Fix: call `20J` the walk-series activity scale, not the cone velocity, and state the `μ`-bound if an exponential LR form is desired.

2. **The iteration cannot literally “apply G3 again” and then discard the self term.**

   Attacked sentence: [G4](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:152>).

   G3’s displayed sum includes every bond touching `supp(h_b)`, including `b`; its self integrand `||[τ_s(h_b),B]||` is not zero. The degree-10 count is legitimate only if `[h_b,h_b]=0` is removed **before Jacobi**, followed by a redefined homogeneous generator
   \[
   \widetilde H_b(t)=\sum_{b'\sim b,\ b'\ne b}\tau_t(h_{b'}).
   \]

   This is a repairable exposition/gating gap, not a counterexample to the walk method. No self-drop is used at the generic first step.

   Attacked claim: frontmatter says “every identity gated”; verification says “sympy-exact … throughout.”

   Examples:

   - G6b checks one factorial pair and a finite partial sum, not the general tail lemma.

1. **The theorem is false for `d=0`, although the hypotheses never exclude it.**

   Attacked sentences: [Hypotheses](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:55>) and “for all `t` and every finite `Λ`” in [G6](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:198>).

   Concrete counterexample: take a two-site region with one bond, `H=Z⊗Z`, `X=Y={1}`, `A=X⊗I`, `B=Y⊗I`. Then `d=0`, `J=1`, `n_X=1`. At `t=0`,
   \[
   \|[A,B]\|=2,
   \]
   while the claimed right side is
   \[
   2(1)(1)\frac1{10}\sum_{k\ge0}0^k/k!=1/5.
   \]

   The N1 assertion that “`d=0` … is outside scope” is not implemented in the hypotheses. Fix: explicitly require `X∩Y=∅`, equivalently `d≥1`, throughout the frontmatter, hypotheses, theorem and runner—or retain the separate `||[A,B]||` term for `d=0`.

2. **The norm-transport lemma is false for negative time as written.**

   Attacked sentence: [G2](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:122>):
   `||f(t)|| ≤ ||f(0)|| + ∫_0^{|t|} ||R(s)|| ds`.

   Concrete counterexample: let `H̃=0`, `f(0)=0`, and
   `R(s)=max(-s,0)I`, which is continuous. At `t=-1`,
   `||f(-1)||=1/2`, but the displayed right side integrates `R(s)=0` over `s∈[0,1]`, giving zero.

   The correct formula is
   \[
   \|f(t)\|\le\|f(0)\|+
   \int_0^{|t|}\|R(\operatorname{sgn}(t)s)\|\,ds,
   \]
   or the proof must be stated for `t≥0` and then repeated for `-H` to obtain the final `|t|` result. The same repair is required in G3. The final all-time series bound remains recoverable because the supplied Hamiltonian class is invariant under `H→-H`.

3. **The runner is false-green on both preceding defects.**

   Attacked gate: [G6e](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/scripts/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py:389>) claims “the `d=0` rejector trips,” but there is no rejector. It merely observes that `{origin}` intersects itself. It never checks that the theorem text declares `d≥1`.

   Likewise, no gate tests the negative-time norm lemma. The runner reports `32 PASS / 0 FAIL` despite both false statements.

   Fix: add an actual domain validator tied to the theorem inputs and a negative-time transport test, then needle the explicit `d≥1` hypothesis.

4. **The stated No-Go Discipline gate does not pass the repo’s mandatory N1–N8 format.**

   Attacked section: [No-Go Discipline Gate](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:227>).

   Specific failures:

   - N1 lacks the required `ATTEMPTED` / `RULED OUT BY PRIOR` markers.
   - N2 compares two expansion mechanisms rather than auditing independence of the supplied conditional hypotheses.
   - N3 misses the hidden `d≥1` condition.
   - N4 is not the required per-citation residual table.
   - N7 misses the strongest counterarguments found above.
   - N8 does not perform the required prior-wall search.
   - No explicit `Status: PASS|FAIL` is recorded.

   Fix: rerun N1–N8 after repairing the theorem. As it stands, the hard gate is `FAIL`.

## MAJOR

1. **`20J` is not the exponential Lieb–Robinson cone velocity claimed in G6.**

   Attacked sentence: [G6](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:206>): “exponential decay outside the cone `|t| ≈ d/(20J)`.”

   The ratio `x/(d+1)<1`, with `x=20J|t|`, only says the displayed factorial majorant decreases as `d` is incremented. It does not give a uniform exponential bound in `d-x`. For example, `x=100`, `d=101>x`, yet the first tail term alone satisfies
   \[
   100^{101}/101!>10^{42}.
   \]

   A valid exponential consequence is, for every `μ>0`,
   \[
   \sum_{k\ge d}\frac{x^k}{k!}
   \le e^{-\mu d+x e^\mu},
   \]
   producing velocity
   \[
   v_\mu=\frac{20J e^\mu}{\mu},
   \]
   minimized at `μ=1` as `20eJ`. Fix: call `20J` the walk-series activity scale, not the cone velocity, and state the `μ`-bound if an exponential LR form is desired.

2. **The iteration cannot literally “apply G3 again” and then discard the self term.**

   Attacked sentence: [G4](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:152>).

   G3’s displayed sum includes every bond touching `supp(h_b)`, including `b`; its self integrand `||[τ_s(h_b),B]||` is not zero. The degree-10 count is legitimate only if `[h_b,h_b]=0` is removed **before Jacobi**, followed by a redefined homogeneous generator
   \[
   \widetilde H_b(t)=\sum_{b'\sim b,\ b'\ne b}\tau_t(h_{b'}).
   \]

   This is a repairable exposition/gating gap, not a counterexample to the walk method. No self-drop is used at the generic first step.

3. **Several universal claims are certified only by single instances.**

   Attacked claim: frontmatter says “every identity gated”; verification says “sympy-exact … throughout.”

   Examples:

   - [G2e](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/scripts/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py:226>) checks one strict triangle-inequality example, not general finite-sum subadditivity.
   - [G4c](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/scripts/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py:303>) hard-codes `J=t=1`; it does not gate `ρ_K→0` for every fixed finite `J,t`.
   - [G5c](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/scripts/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py:323>) checks `100` continuations for only the first start bond, although the note says “each.”
   - G6b checks one factorial pair and a finite partial sum, not the general tail lemma.

   Fix: either add proof-level symbolic/property gates or describe these honestly as instance checks supporting the written analytical proof.

4. **The claimed committed runner cache does not exist.**

   Attacked sentences: [header](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:26>) and [Verification](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:299>).

   The referenced `logs/runner-cache/microcausality_all_time_volume_uniform_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt` is absent and untracked. Fix: create and commit the SHA-pinned transcript or remove both assertions.

   Attacked headings begin at [G1](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:83>).

   Attacked sentence: [G7](</Users/jonBridger/Toy Physics/.claude/worktrees/quirky-wiles-92e3b4/docs/MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md:224>).

## Claims that survive

- **G1 survives:** the Jacobi identity, signs, conjugation bookkeeping and boundary reduction are correct.
- **G2’s ODE setup survives for directed time:** Hermiticity, self-adjointness of `H̃(t)`, differentiability and propagator existence are declared; `W=V†` and `W'=-iWH̃` are legitimate.
- **Self-drop survives when performed before Jacobi:** it is used only in the bond-to-bond iterations, not smuggled into the generic first step.
- **Walk bookkeeping survives:** `6`, `10`, `60`, `100`; length `k` means `k` bonds and `k−1` adjacency steps; `k≥d`; induced-region transfer; and `n_X` correctly counts bonds in `E(Λ)`.
- **Series bookkeeping survives for `d≥1`:** the `(2J)^{k-1}` base, terminal `2J||B||`, coefficient identity and factorial remainder are correct.
- **Tail arithmetic survives:** independently,
  `3^200·200^800/800! ≈ 2.297×10^-41 < 10^-40`.
- **Sibling comparisons are factually fair:** block01 really uses `2J|E(Λ)|`; block02’s stated instance window is `1/12`. Neither sibling is contradicted.
- **SymPy truth handling survives:** both `.is_positive` uses require `is True`; `None` therefore fails rather than silently passing. No `.is_nonnegative` hazard exists.
- **Scope boundary largely survives:** `H` is explicitly supplied, the axioms provide no dynamics, and literature is comparator-only.

Final recommendation: **NEEDS REVISION.** After the `d≥1`, negative-time, self-drop exposition, cone-rate and runner repairs, the central result is a credible bounded theorem; no new physics input is needed.
